# LoL2 port: deviations ledger

Every intentional difference from the original game, for owner approval (process rule, 2026-10-20).
Status: **pending** (awaiting the owner), **approved**, **rejected** (to be reverted).

| Date | Area | Change | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2026-10-20 | All | The picture uses 4x even integer scaling. | Sharp pixels on modern screens. | pending |

## Owner requests answered from the original data (no deviation)

- **2026-10-21, cave shrine blocks (report 2026-10-20 18:53, "should be able to move them").**
  - In the original the player cannot push them.
  - Each block's only script is a contact event that fires for actor38, the colonnade guard. The contact walker is at AE7D8; case 6 needs the other object to be actor 38.
  - The guard appears when the player enters region1941. When he chases the player and touches a block, both blocks slide into the side niches. The longplay shows them closed at 9:05 and open at 9:35.
  - CORRECTED 2026-10-21: the longplay at 4 fps (9:04–9:10) shows the blocks parting as the human player presses in,
    with no guard in view (Codex witness b_shrine.jpg). The port now also opens them on player contact (see the
    ledger above).
| 2026-10-22 | Dark Halls | Doors 56/57 (template16): E within 120 units toggles them (native use records: shut -> open, open -> shut); the leaf swings 120 degrees on its native hinge corner in 1 s and its collider is the native template box. Door 57's trap is hosted: a grounded entry into region 1338 slams and locks it and moves grid SHADE 73 beside the trap; the SHADE's death (native kind6 value10) unlocks it. Not hosted: breaking a door with three hits, and region 83 locking door 56. | Owner 2026-10-22: continue, modernize. | pending |

| 2026-10-23 | Jungle | Walls and edges drawn the way the original portal renderer shows them: walls are one-sided (seen only from their own region); boundary walls without a wall texture, floorless regions (floor selector 255) and the backs and see-through pixels of solid boundary walls show the original panorama and hide what lies beyond (the portal walk stops at solid edges). Emulation: a backdrop material with the sky projection on those faces, pushed 0.1% behind its plane in view space so textured walls on the same plane win. Before, walls were two-sided, so the black walls of the imp pit (material 744, a flat palette-3 texture) stood in the jungle as black blocks, the marble-looking back of a wall showed as a block, the village showed through foliage walls and floorless regions were a flat grey-green plane. Not reproduced: native per-portal clipping (whole regions behind open edges are drawn); one interior foliage span seen edge-on still shows as a thin vine strip (report 44, needs a native comparison). | Owner playtest 2026-10-23, reports 33, 35, 45, 47, 48, 49. | pending |

Assumed: the original never shows the panorama at eye level there; its longplay frames show foliage walls at every jungle edge, and the original renderer's behaviour for a record-less edge is not traced. | 2026-10-25 | Jungle | Causeway collapse (prop4648 event20, g17352, called by all three exit endings before their movie): 12 causeway floors sink to -200 (preset 42, water) and region4445 by 20; the changed walls swap; controls 61/62 link and control84 unlinks (its native solid, a 60-high panel, collides until then); regions 4429/4448/4447 refuse creatures after the collapse (region bit1, the shared creature_region_barrier adapter; the player is never refused); local43 = 1 is held as the saved collapse. Verified from source: the commands, regions, targets and presets (scene regenerated by the published builder) and the call from each ending. Modern: floors are shown settled at once (native speeds 244/25 settle in about 0.1/0.3 s) and the collapse is applied when the ending queues it, before the movie, so movie end and skip leave the same world. A save that holds a played ending but no causeway entry loads collapsed. The eight scripted causeway props (template39, original sprite 755) are drawn by this host: 507/509/4162 unlink and 4161/508/506/4181/4160 teleport to their op18 x/z at their own height (native 65BB0: flags 0x0a85 teleport, height only with bit 0x100); whether the native mover runs for these props is not traced. Content gaps, not hosted: the shake and movables 59/60/62/63 (not drawn by the port); the floor tests do not cover these effects. | Owner r12e F10 2026-10-25 11:05 ("the brigge is not destroyed after the tour of bakata"); Codex causeway_source_check.json. | pending |
| 2026-10-25 | Jungle | The river gorge (22 regions 3314-3335, floor -500, walls to 400) runs under the south bank floors (regions 1902/1905 at 0); its 18 walls above the top of its only openings (the waterfall openings from the channel, header bottom -10) are not drawn (collision unchanged). Rule, derived from the geometry: regions joined across openings without a header form a group; a group whose outer walls pass through another group's floor is drawn up to its highest opening top. Only this group qualifies in the Jungle (none in the Darker Jungle). Emulates the portal clip: the original reaches the gorge only through those openings, and the longplay (36:40-38:10) shows no wall above the bank there. Not reproduced: from the drained channel right at the waterfall, the original would show more of the far gorge walls through the opening. Before, the backs of the walls stood 400 units above the bank as backdrop. | Owner r12e F10 2026-10-25 11:02 ("a big hole in the world", at 3153,-4415). | pending |

### Jungle wooden plank bridge support and creature refusal (2026-10-25)

The source beast-only support-entry break on plank210 is hosted separately from the Bacatta causeway. The existing
four-unit grounded support adapter replaces the original vertical support solver. Selector1 removes deck faces;
the original whole-box collision remains. Assembly211 and five original debris sprites link on break, with original
sound requests. Creature-only region3032 refusal uses outline collision walls on layer11; player movement and aim
rays ignore them, and creatures already inside can leave. Quest state restores the one-time break and support history
without replaying sound. See `jungle-bridge-break.md` for source pins, media preparation and verification limits.

| 2026-10-29 | Cave | Classic depth shading: palette rows from the level's own shade table by the native 0x1319B4 distance/ambient formula (per region: ambient, fade start, span). Adapters: (1) moving sprites and placed objects without static region data use the viewer's region sector, not their own; (2) the surface multiplier [0xFD8A8] is 128 (neutral in mult*(x>>7)) because its per-surface source byte is untraced; (3) run-time light records (torches, spells, the [0xFD8C0] global term) are not added yet; (4) a sprite pixel shaded to index 0 is written as index 3 (the same palette black) so the layer compositor keeps it opaque; (5) every wall, floor and ceiling interpolates the per-vertex returns (G47: with both 0x126F10/0x126F21 flags clear the original either fills the span with the row-0 byte, black, when every return is below row 1, or joins the same 0x129DA0 interpolation; its column x origin [0xFE434]/[0xFE438] stays untraced); (6) the sector uses the base 0xE2E40 store branch (file ambient, fade start, span), not the alternate branch (ambient + 0x14, fade start x 5), whose gate is run-time; (7) screen-linear interpolation is done as (level*w, w) under perspective-correct varyings, since Godot has no noperspective; (8) distance is the float length capped at 32767 then truncated (native: fsqrt of the integer squares, then 0x158EDE round toward zero); (9) the interpolated row is clamped to 0..63 (native clamps the endpoint only). Museum, Jungle and the other levels are not shaded yet (RGB materials). | Native look (owner: Cave too bright); terms not traced stay neutral or off until DOSBox capture (G38). | pending |

Without it the host keys command writes by the raw address. | Owner rule 2026-10-30 (workaround when it plays as intended). | pending |
| 2026-10-30 | All (native script host) | Adapters b5000_bit_clear and b71a0_bit_clear: object byte +0x15 bits 0x80 and 0x10 are taken as clear when not known (the constructors' value of +0x15 is not decoded). Bit 0x80 set would skip the kind-4 walk (0xB5000); bit 0x10 set would stop 0xB71A0 from storing the object as the kind-4 context and from returning its event list (opcode 14, opcode 9 arm 3). Known bits (written by opcode 9) decide without the adapter. | Owner rule 2026-10-30. | pending |

### 2026-10-30 — L9_DR map preview (A01, adapter; OPEN)

The preview uses native arrival selector 0 at (3607, -12, -8471) in Godot coordinates. Its campaign-entry label is UNKNOWN; heading zero is an explicit camera adapter, not a conversion of source heading 24003. The map has no progression scripts yet. Original panorama cavebk.pcx uses native vertical origin -100 through the existing sky projection adapter.

The 80 source-wall records whose neighbor is absent remain omitted. Their visible original behavior is UNKNOWN pending a location-matched longplay witness; no boundary geometry is invented. Supported source walls are one-sided toward their owner region and draw only, without collision. Native bit0 blocker data is exported but disabled in this preview unless walk evidence establishes a need. Region floors/ceilings and supported native assembly boxes supply collision; scripted assembly collision remains with future script hosts. The 47-record region/floor difference is ceiling children and an owner represented by child floors, not missing floors.

The 36 region-less props stay excluded under the existing native spawn-lookup rule. Initial billboard frames use source placement and flip bits; animation rates remain UNKNOWN. Native progression, movable state changes and the 19 alternate-state attached props are not activated by this map preview.

### 2026-10-31 — Astra connected level arrivals (adapter)

Act 2/3 transitions now consume the native selector in L8 and L9 as well as the generic host. L8 portable packs retain all decoded arrivals (regenerate with prepare_darker_jungle_portable.py). Generic arrival height uses the selected region's minimum decoded floor +32, rather than the unrelated preview floor. L8/L9 retain the positions emitted by their existing producer, including its +48 arrival height convention; linear heading-to-yaw remains the existing camera adapter. Coordinates, region and selector are source data; camera clearance and yaw are adapters, not PROVEN original camera behavior. No shared jungle_review rule changes.

The shared Act 2 binder initializes its region watcher at the placed arrival and fires entry only after the player changes region. This prevents L14 selector 3 (region 4176, also a level-exit owner) from immediately teleporting back. This placement-versus-crossing policy is an adapter; original initialization semantics remain UNKNOWN. L9 uses the same binder and hinge exclusion as generic maps, with existing source-wall/terrain collision policy retained. Unsupported exit groups still report their actual unhosted reasons; no predicate or command is skipped to force travel.

### 2026-10-31 — Act 2/3 connected-world input and session adapters (Astra)

`act2_travel.json` pins all41 decoded exits and all arrival records to original archive offsets/bytes; the scene mapping and arrival fields remain LIKELY per Grok backlog5 items1/2. The Act2 binder offers E on exit controls/props/movables within120 units, facing0.7 and unobstructed. Original control/prop callers remain UNKNOWN; this modern input supplies the record's caller bytes, runs the existing matcher and full group, and never overwrites predicates/record bits to force an exit. Unknown/rejected paths remain unavailable. L8's return to L4 uses a separate Act2 return host, preserving the shared Act1 base.

During scene travel the binder carries the existing validated player handoff and caches native state/locals/second-array/rewritten records per area in the SceneTree. This is a port session adapter, not an original save format or proof of global/local lifetime. The initial stage supplied no unknown campaign values; the separately named arrival-only adapters below supersede that restriction under the lead's14:57:13Z authorization. The travel graph diagnostic reports unreachable/blocked edges; its `--maps-complete` gate fails unless every exit runs and every arrival has nearby floor contact with no bounce. Graph traversal is event-driven scene travel, not proof of an uninterrupted spatial walk through every corridor.

### 2026-10-31 — L9/L20 below-panorama stripe repair (adapter)

Old L9 arrival2 captures showed vertical panorama stripes. With cavebk vertical_origin=-100, the projected y is at or below300 and the prior shader clamped to the final row (26 differing colours repeated vertically). L9/L20 hosts now opt into filling below-image samples with the original panorama's first pixel instead. Native below-image framebuffer retention remains UNKNOWN/unreproduced; this is a modern clear-colour fallback, not a claimed native projection fix. The optional shader parameter defaults OFF; no Act1 host or shared jungle_review rule changes. New rendered comparison still requires a renderer grant.

### 2026-10-31 — Act 2/3 movable geometry and collision (Astra adapter)

The shared Act2 binder now owns mesh faces for all decoded opcode1 movable targets and kind4 movable owners, including L8. This replaces the prior kind4/mode6-only subset, so region-driven movers are not left in the static shell. Each moving mesh has an AnimatableBody3D concave collision shape; the original collision shape and motion timing are not claimed. Native script handlers remain Fable's shared host: refused groups do not move geometry. Mode6 uses the existing travel-word yaw adapter; non6 motion uses a geometry-derived fallback (vertical panels retract upward by their height+8, horizontal platforms slide along placement heading by their longest extent+8, scaled by the accepted travel word). These axes/distances are modern adapters, not decoded original door/lift/bridge identities. Interpolation approaches the target at factor4*delta; a player standing on the platform is carried through its transform. Models without exported initial faces remain absent and must be listed, not fabricated. Newly observed field writes drive poses; per-area native state is retained by the world session adapter. Source opcode1 commits are checked by per-level tests, and unsupported handlers remain failures rather than being bypassed.

### 2026-10-31 — Act2/3 source creature populations (Astra STEP6 adapters)
All ten hosts use scripted_creature_population, source actor coordinates/heading/presence and decoded original indexed sprites. B_HUMAN invisible script emitters are explicitly counted and excluded from creature bodies. Placement health is preserved; zero values use 30 health pending constructor evidence. Damage uses borrowed base15 with source action5 hit percentages. Generic perception/steering/clocks and a 48-unit silhouette height/capsule are adapters, not original stats. Missing action clips fall back to an explicitly recorded decoded selector. Named story NPCs stay dormant/peaceful until a native wake or attack, pending their dialogue owners. Actors targeted by source opcode13 wake/sleep groups begin dormant; the shared host's accepted +0xB5 bit0 writes map clear->awake, set->asleep (LIKELY behavior adapter; the stores themselves PROVEN). No group/predicate bypass. Unsupported sprites are recorded and fail census qualification. Population packets live in per-area quest keys and restore through the generic validator; Act1 hosts are unchanged. Native faction, spells, boss phases and loot drops remain unhosted.

Act2 hosts use a separate compact atomic save writer with a 4MiB guard for ten populations, validate every Act2 packet before mutating the host, and restore their owner on F9/handoff. Existing Act1 save limits/admission are unchanged. This is port persistence, not a claim about DOS save layout.

### Opcode18 direct relocation evidence update — 2026-11-03

Probrogit PR #9 is applicable to our LOLG.DAT SHA256 b27a35341c6e877e40b1540b6482748e7a750c605fefe6452431b18a5d766775: Grok’s `~/AI_COMMS/grok_research/opcode18_b27a3534.md` verifies identical routine bytes at VA[0x65BB0,0x6612E), SHA25677b2a6c2abfb74152467bd8819569ba7d18f0d4d6b6f3752de2b07b00a89c242, and the same call path. This is static same-build evidence, not a new Unicorn or gameplay replay. Flags are u16 at record+10;0x0004 selects native direct relocation; signed+4/+6 give horizontal destination, +8 assigns height only with0x0100, and+12 gives heading.0x0020 marks completion. Trigger admission, floor grounding, presentation and persistence can still be host adapters. No blanket claim about gradual motion follows.

City elevator STORY067: region233/descriptor2/group564 runs movie27 then kind1 raw120100002bf10df7000004080080000a0000, flags0x0804. This is native direct relocation to(-3797,-2291), heading0x8000;0x0100 is clear, so raw height0 is not an explicit assignment. Destination source floor343 is0. Gameplay remains unimplemented/unqualified; physical doorway/movie admission and host grounding still need implementation, not an invented moving platform.

Nonmatching records remain unresolved: Citadel kind1 flags0x0293 lacks0x0004; Temple moving-stair kind0x10 flags0x0081 lacks0x0004. The Wild Village SHAMAN place call is not opcode18. Neither the direct-relocation finding nor matching coordinate words prove these separate execution modes.

The bank-model surfaces are an adapter: the native liquid level bytes are not decoded; the dam (movables 70-72/50 travel 100 on local 2) and the switch shots stay with Grok's BK05/BK06, which can move the cavity surface through liquid_drowning.regions when hosted. tests/l19_guck_test.gd. | Adapter (owner workaround rule 2026-10-30) |
| 2026-11-09 | Region-aware collision: only the regions near the player's collide (L17, L19) | The original's mover is region-based: the player's position is a region, and movement reads only that region's sides and neighbours, so two region layouts laid through one space (L17's corridor 1236 over corridor 562 at the same floor, the automap's "Layer" system) never touch each other's walls, floors or ceilings. The port's one static collision mesh collided everything. jungle_review now keeps the static collision per owning region (one CollisionShape3D per region under "RegionCollision"; faces without a region stay always on) when region_collision_enabled, and act2_map (REGION_COLLISION_AREAS = L17_HC, L19_BC) enables only the regions within COLLISION_HOPS = 8 nav hops of the player's region (links: the creature nav's stored edges plus polygon sides shared within 48 of floor, region_edge_rule.links). The player's region is tracked like the original's: it stays while the body is inside its polygon, leaves only into a linked neighbour, and is placed afresh only when it lies in neither (progression.region_at), so an overlapping layout never captures the body. The set is refreshed from _physics_process, from move_grounded (the harness walks run with scene physics off) and after apply_save. | BK01 follow-up (Fable; lead decision 2026-11-09: approved as an adapter restoring the original region-based mover, scoped to L17_HC/L19_BC first). The hop count is a port choice (8 keeps the whole visible neighbourhood solid; 1236 is 17 hops from 562); the native mover needs none. tests/l17_throat_walk_test.gd: arrival 1 -> cave throat -> exit pocket on foot, no fall recovery (with the Human's chain-save gear, running past the spiders). Other areas keep the single collision mesh until walked with it. | Adapter (collision scoped by the native region graph) Follow-up (Kimi K62): the act2 save records the body's tracked region (optional player.region) and apply_save keeps it while the body still lies inside that polygon, so a save taken where two layouts overlap (the L17 throat) reloads on the same layer; a save without the key stays valid (l17_throat_save_test). |

The body there stands on the floor (feet 0.14 over it), but the floor slopes: the lizard is a flat-based cylinder (radius 8, height 8 - a capsule cannot be 8 by 8 in Godot) and the rim of its base reached into the rising side of the ground (contact at (-352.7, -62.7)); the human capsule's rounded base does not. player_form_body.gd apply(): for the lizard, with the body on the floor (or a restored pose), the space check starts one lizard radius (8, a 45-degree floor) above the feet - capped one unit under its height - so its sides and its top keep full clearance and a roof lower than the lizard still refuses. Human and beast checks unchanged. | form_fit_slope_test (rendered, real input: F2 act2-l12, 3 + Ctrl+Q at once -> lizard; the same stand under a roof slab 6 over the feet -> refused); red on r19-work 0d13144 ('no effect here'). Forms/curse/body-fit set 28/28. | Fix. |
| 2026-11-14 | Act 2/3 maps: a load places Luther in his region without its entry event, and re-arms only the entry's repeat-safe commands | Fix (lead 2026-11-14, generalising row 0659 after the census: about 1000 region entry events in L8-L20, nearly all holding a command that is not safe to repeat). The original's save holds its world whole and its loader sets the player's region directly; the port's area save does not hold everything (listed kind-2 timers, effects, room/ambience), and the next physics tick counted the loaded region as entered and re-ran its entry group - a lever press (the L19 cab turned back), a pose, a movie, a mover or actor started twice. scripts/lol2/load_region_rule.gd (called at the end of apply_save on act2_map, darker_jungle, dragon_level, act2_jungle_return; areas L4/L8/L9/L10/L12/L13/L14/L16/L17/L19/L20): the progression's region is set where the save put the body, then native_script_host.rearm_region matches the region's entry records and predicates as on entry, makes no record write, and runs only the commands on the REARM table - opcode 12 (room/ambience), 14 (timers), 15 (effects), 196/197/198 (absolute writes). Every other entry opcode is on the NEVER table (levers/enqueues, poses, movies, counters, items, actors, movers; 199/204 are absolute writes left for a decision); the census test fails on an opcode on neither list. Replaces the L19-only block of row 0659. Old saves load unchanged (no format change). | load_region_rule_test (headless census: 27 entry opcodes in 15 levels each on one list; L19 1943 re-arm keeps opcode 197, drops the lever press, enqueues nothing); load_region_rule_input_test (rendered, real F5/F9 in L10 yard 247 and L14 2546: no entry again, no enqueue, a re-arm row; red on r19-work 9d1061c - region 247's entry ran again with its opcode-9 enqueue); l19_cab_ride_save_test (the L19 case). | Fix. Saved state wins (lead after Grok R118): a re-arm command whose target the loaded save already holds is skipped (dry run per command); L4 region 1654 keeps record 3805 bit 0 cleared by control 82; census 574 re-arm writes skipped, 130 still written (effects, timers, room requests). |
| 2026-11-15 | L10 Cemetery: thin hanging regions that no body can stand in are solid to the player | Fix (owner F10 2026-11-15 03-27-37, lead scope the same day). A region whose ceiling is less than 16 above its floor (the smallest body) holds no body; where one hangs over walkable ground with less than a standing body (46) between them, a body on that ground slid under its floor face and was wedged (yard region 1029, floor 35 / ceiling 45 over the yard at 0: feet 7 into the floor, every direction blocked; the same on r19g, r19l, r19n). The native walls on such a region's edges cover only its own height. tools/prepare_thin_region_blocks.py lists them from the pack (scripts/lol2/thin_region_blocks/L10_DC.json, pinned to the moved pack's sha256; 31 regions, all 35/45); scripts/lol2/thin_region_blocks.gd builds one convex prism per floor polygon from 1 above the ground under it to its ceiling on the region-blocker layer (player only), and lifts a body already inside one out onto that ground on the nearest free side (old saves). Regions with floor == ceiling (closed and door regions) are left out. L10 only; other levels counted, not built. | l10_thin_region_input_test (rendered: real W along the wall turned aside at h 32, real S walks away; a body placed at the owner's trapped pose lifted onto the yard, real S/A move it; red on r19n 7ca0961 at h 25.11). | Fix. |

| 2026-11-15 | Citadel (L13_RC) pink stair: plate tops collide only over their own tread; a body left hanging on a tread edge is stepped off it | Fix (Grok's owner-save replay of 04-56-17 on r19o, 2026-11-15 12:07Z; lead scope the same day). With the one-sided plate tops of row 0651, a jump from one pink tread can rise into the plate above and leave the capsule hanging on the next tread's outer edge (floor face 1509 / riser 3980 at tread 848): the contact is almost vertical, so there is no floor, the jump key is dead and nothing is shown. Two parts. (1) Overhang trim: each plate top collides only over its own tread (the one 22 below it), not where it hangs over the tread beneath, so the jump from that lower tread no longer rises into it; the drawing is unchanged. (2) Safety net: when the body is airborne on the pink stair and has not moved for half a second, it is moved 4 units off that edge (away from the contacts) and falls back onto the tread below, so the next jump can be made. Nothing is drawn or said; the treads, plates and risers are unchanged. Proof (lead standard 14:33Z): l13_pink_owner_input_test, treads 2-20 each within three jumps from a standing start, from the owner save AND the actual F2 pink-base slot, with and without Shift (red on 787c707 at the owner's spot; here every tread lands on the first jump, 0 releases); l13_pink_ledge_input_test now starts the real way by default (F2 + slot; LOL2_PINK_BASE=0 keeps the old Dawn-direct load), red on 787c707, 21/21 here. |
